SEC. 1133. PERMITTING IMPROVEMENT FUND.  6
(a) E STABLISHMENT.—There is established in the  7
Treasury of the United States a fund, to be known as the  8
‘‘Permitting Improvement Fund’’ (referred to in this sec -9
tion as the ‘‘Fund’’).  10
(b) P URPOSE.—The purpose of the Fund is to im -11
prove the performance of key permitting agencies with re -12
spect to timely environmental reviews and authorizations  13
required under the National Environmental Policy Act of  14
1969 (42 U.S.C. 4321 et seq.). 15
(c) T RANSFER AUTHORITY.—Amounts in the Fund  16
shall be available to the Chairman to transfer to a key  17
permitting agency for 1 or more uses described in sub -18
section (d). 19
(d) U SE OF AMOUNTS.—Amounts transferred under  20
subsection (c) shall be used by a key permitting agency—  21
(1) to support interagency detailee and rotation  22
opportunities; 23
(2) to facilitate professional development;  24
===== PAGE 102 =====
102 
KAT26642 TWY  S.L.C. 
(3) to enhance support for agency project man -1
agers; 2
(4) to support standardizing processes among  3
regional and field offices;  4
(5) to improve opportunities for public engage -5
ment; or 6
(6) for any other use, as determined by the  7
Chairman, consistent with the purpose of the Fund.  8
(e) EFFECT ON DECISIONMAKING.—The use of funds 9
under subsection (d) by a key permitting agency shall not  10
impact the impartial decisionmaking of the key permitting  11
agency with respect to environmental reviews or authoriza -12
tions, either procedurally or substantively. 13
(f) A UTHORIZATION OF APPROPRIATION.—There is  14
authorized to be appropriated to the Fund $20,000,000  15
for each of fiscal years 2027 through 2033. 16

SEC. 1308. NATIONAL FISH AND WILDLIFE FOUNDATION  1
COASTAL STATE FUND. 2
(a) DEFINITIONS.—In this section: 3
(1) C OASTAL COUNTY .—The term ‘‘coastal  4
county’’ has the meaning given the term by the Na -5
tional Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration in  6
the document entitled ‘‘NOAA’s List of Coastal  7
Counties for the Bureau of the Census’’ (or a simi -8
lar successor document).  9
(2) C OASTAL STATE .—The term ‘‘coastal  10
State’’ has the meaning given the term ‘‘coastal  11
state’’ in section 304 of the Coastal Zone Manage -12
ment Act of 1972 (16 U.S.C. 1453).  13
(3) F OUNDATION.—The term ‘‘Foundation’’  14
means the National Fish and Wildlife Foundation  15
established by section 2(a) of the National Fish and  16
Wildlife Foundation Establishment Act (16 U.S.C.  17
3701(a)). 18
(4) P ROGRAM.—The term ‘‘program’’ means  19
the National Fish and Wildlife Foundation Coastal  20
State Fund established under subsection (b).  21
(5) TIDAL SHORELINE .—The term ‘‘tidal shore -22
line’’ means the length of tidal shoreline or Great  23
Lake shoreline based on the most recently available  24
data from or accepted by the Office of Coast Survey  25
===== PAGE 157 =====
157 
KAT26642 TWY  S.L.C. 
of the National Oceanic and Atmospheric Adminis -1
tration. 2
(b) E STABLISHMENT OF PROGRAM.—Not later than  3
1 year after the date of enactment of this Act, subject  4
to the availability of appropriations, the Foundation shall  5
establish and carry out a program, to be known as the  6
‘‘National Fish and Wildlife Foundation Coastal State  7
Fund’’, to award grants to coastal States to support State  8
programs and activities intended to better understand and  9
use coastal resources, coastal and natural infrastructure,  10
and ecosystem services provided by natural systems. 11
(c) A LLOCATION OF AMOUNTS.—Of the amounts  12
made available to carry out the program each fiscal year,  13
subject to subsection (d), such amounts shall be allocated  14
to grants for coastal States under the program in accord -15
ance with the following: 16
(1) 70 percent shall be allocated equally among  17
coastal States. 18
(2) 15 percent shall be allocated on the basis of  19
the ratio of tidal shoreline in a coastal State to the  20
tidal shoreline of all coastal States.  21
(3) 15 percent shall be allocated on the basis of  22
the ratio of population density of the coastal coun -23
ties of a coastal State to the average population den -24
===== PAGE 158 =====
158 
KAT26642 TWY  S.L.C. 
sity of all coastal counties based on the most recent  1
data available from the Bureau of the Census.  2
(d) LIMITATION FOR SINGLE COASTAL STATE.—Not-3
withstanding subsection (c), not more than 5 percent of  4
the total amounts allocated under that subsection may be  5
allocated to any single coastal State, and any amount ex -6
ceeding that limitation shall be reallocated equally among  7
the remaining coastal States. 8
(e) FUNDING.— 9
(1) A UTHORIZATION OF APPROPRIATIONS .— 10
There is authorized to be appropriated to carry out  11
the program $50,000,000 for each of fiscal years  12
2028 through 2032.  13
(2) A DDITIONAL FUNDING .—In addition to the  14
amounts made available pursuant to paragraph (1),  15
the Foundation may use such additional amounts as  16
may be made available to carry out the program.  17

(a) S ENSE OF CONGRESS.—It is the sense of Con -13

(1) all Federal agencies shall treat all applica -15

TING. 8

===== PAGE 183 =====

mental impact statement or environmental assessment (as  1

(d) R EMEDY.—If a court of competent jurisdiction  11

(a) FERC P RICING POLICY FOR TRANSMISSION 16

MITTING UTILITIES.— 18
(1) FINDINGS.—Congress finds that—  19
(A) it is not just and reasonable within the  20
meaning of sections 205 and 206 of the Federal  21
Power Act (16 U.S.C. 824d, 824e) for any pub -22
lic utility or transmitting utility to maintain  23

===== PAGE 227 =====

or avoid the cost burden on other customers;  8
and 9
(C) computational load owners and com -10
putational load operators should incur the full  11
cost of the transmission service they require.  12

SEC. 2109. ADVANCED TRANSMISSION TECHNOLOGIES.  1
(a) IN GENERAL.—Part II of the Federal Power Act  2

is amended by adding at the end the following: 4
‘‘SEC. 231. USE OF ADVANCED TRANSMISSION TECH -5
NOLOGIES. 6
‘‘(a) D EFINITIONS.—In this section: 7
‘‘(1) A DVANCED TRANSMISSION TECH -8
NOLOGY.—The term ‘advanced transmission tech -9
nology’ has the meaning given the term in section  10
1223(a) of the Energy Policy Act of 2005 (42  11

‘‘(2) B EST-AVAILABLE TRANSMISSION CON -13
DUCTOR.—The term ‘best-available transmission  14
conductor’ means a transmission conductor that, as  15
determined by the Commission—  16
‘‘(A) provides the greatest feasible and  17
commercially available energy-carrying capacity  18
at a given voltage level;  19
‘‘(B) provides the highest feasible and  20
commercially available electrical efficiency at  21
that voltage level; and  22
‘‘(C) provides the greatest available level of  23
mechanical performance, including mitigating  24
thermal sag at the maximum rated trans -25
===== PAGE 261 =====
261 
KAT26642 TWY  S.L.C. 
mission-carrying capacity of the applicable  1
transmission facility. 2
‘‘(3) C OMMISSION.—The term ‘Commission’  3

‘‘(4) C OVERED PROJECT .—The term ‘covered  5
project’ means the construction of a new trans -6
mission facility, or the modification, upgrade, re -7
placement, or reconductoring of an existing trans -8
mission facility, subject to the jurisdiction of the  9
Commission under section 201(b).  10
‘‘(b) U SE OF ADVANCED TRANSMISSION TECH-11
NOLOGIES.—In exercising its authority under sections 205  12
and 206, the Commission shall require that public utilities  13
use advanced transmission technologies, including best-  14
available transmission conductors, when carrying out cov -15
ered projects. 16
‘‘(c) P RESUMPTION OF PRUDENCE FOR ADVANCED 17
TRANSMISSION TECHNOLOGIES.—In any filing seeking to  18
recover the cost of an advanced transmission technology  19
for a covered project, and in any proceeding to determine  20
whether that cost may be recovered through rates, the  21

‘‘(1) the use of an advanced transmission tech -23
nology is a prudent practice; and  24
===== PAGE 262 =====

KAT26642 TWY  S.L.C. 
‘‘(2) the costs associated with the use of an ad -1
vanced transmission technology are just and reason -2
able. 3
‘‘(d) A PPLICABILITY.—The requirements of this sec -4
tion apply only to public utilities and only with respect  5
to covered projects. 6
‘‘(e) R ULEMAKING.— 7
‘‘(1) I N GENERAL .—Not later than 1 year after  8
the date of enactment of this section, the Commis -9
sion shall issue an order or rule to implement this  10
section. 11
‘‘(2) R EQUIREMENTS.—In issuing an order or  12
rule under paragraph (1), the Commission shall—  13
‘‘(A) require that public utilities conduct,  14
on Commission-established timelines, recurring  15
evaluations— 16
‘‘(i) to determine whether the benefits  17
associated with deploying advanced trans -18
mission technologies through 1 or more  19
covered projects, including benefits to the  20
current transmission system of the applica -21
ble public utility, outweigh the costs (such  22
that the applicable benefit-cost ratio is  23

===== PAGE 263 =====

KAT26642 TWY  S.L.C. 
‘‘(ii) that include a least-cost analysis  1

‘‘(iii) that evaluate whether the costs  4
for which recovery is sought reflect due  5

cable covered projects;  7
‘‘(B) require public utilities to conduct, on  8

‘‘(C) require that, if the benefits of 1 or  13

‘‘(D) require that decisions on whether to  20

‘‘(2) does not include a project that impounds  19

SEC. 2252. OFFSHORE ENERGY TRANSMISSION.  1
(a) DEFINITIONS.—In this section: 2
(1) B ACKBONE LINE .—The term ‘‘backbone  3
line’’ means a transmission line that connects mul -4

(2) FEDERAL AUTHORIZATION.— 6
(A) I N GENERAL .—The term ‘‘Federal au -7
thorization’’ means any authorization required  8

transmission facility. 10
(B) I NCLUSIONS.—The term ‘‘Federal au -11
thorization’’ includes such permits, special use  12

in order to site an offshore transmission facil -15
ity. 16
(3) O FFSHORE GENERATION FACILITY .—The 17

(A) is located on the outer Continental  20
Shelf; and 21
(B) produces electric energy.  22
(4) OFFSHORE TRANSMISSION FACILITY .— 23
